Whatever dispatches `Write`\n=== PR ===\ntitle:\tRequire tool-result evidence before general-change completion\nstate:\tDRAFT\nauthor:\tkonard (Konstantin Diachenko)\nlabels:\t\nassignees:\tkonard (Konstantin Diachenko)\nreviewers:\t\nprojects:\t\nmilestone:\t\nnumber:\t927\nurl:\thttps://github.com/link-assistant/formal-ai/pull/927\nadditions:\t10806\ndeletions:\t1051\nauto-merge:\tdisabled\n--\n## Summary\n\n- preserve explicit tool failure metadata across OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic, and Responses inputs\n- separate observed attempts from successful planner steps, recover one rejected write with read + bounded retry, preserve the primary write when an auxiliary plan write fails, and stop honestly on repeated or verification failures\n- require successful verification output to match the requested bytes before reporting completion\n- seed mismatch/unverified responses in all five supported languages and refresh generated closure/self-AST evidence\n\nCloses #905.\n\n## Reproduction and root cause\n\nThe reported prompt, `Create a file hello.txt containing exactly: Hello World`, reproduced three defects: the shorter equal-start marker left `exactly:` in the payload; provider error metadata was discarded while every tool result counted as completed; and the general planner emitted success based on call counts without checking result status or output.\n\nThe pre-fix focused suite retained in `docs/case-studies/issue-905/test-evidence/regression-red.log` fails 6 of 7 assertions. Both original Agent CLI provider traces and authenticated issue/PR snapshots are retained in the case study. Whatever dispatches `Write`\n=== PR ===\ntitle:\tRequire tool-result evidence before general-change completion\nstate:\tDRAFT\nauthor:\tkonard (Konstantin Diachenko)\nlabels:\t\nassignees:\tkonard (Konstantin Diachenko)\nreviewers:\t\nprojects:\t\nmilestone:\t\nnumber:\t927\nurl:\thttps://github.com/link-assistant/formal-ai/pull/927\nadditions:\t10806\ndeletions:\t1051\nauto-merge:\tdisabled\n--\n## Summary\n\n- preserve explicit tool failure metadata across OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic, and Responses inputs\n- separate observed attempts from successful planner steps, recover one rejected write with read + bounded retry, preserve the primary write when an auxiliary plan write fails, and stop honestly on repeated or verification failures\n- require successful verification output to match the requested bytes before reporting completion\n- seed mismatch/unverified responses in all five supported languages and refresh generated closure/self-AST evidence\n\nCloses #905.\n\n## Reproduction and root cause\n\nThe reported prompt, `Create a file hello.txt containing exactly: Hello World`, reproduced three defects: the shorter equal-start marker left `exactly:` in the payload; provider error metadata was discarded while every tool result counted as completed; and the general planner emitted success based on call counts without checking result status or output.\n\nThe pre-fix focused suite retained in `docs/case-studies/issue-905/test-evidence/regression-red.log` fails 6 of 7 assertions. 🔍 Searching for created pull requests or comments...

🔍 Checking for pull requests from branch issue-905-84e37855d352...
  ✅ Found pull request #927: "Require tool-result evidence before general-change completion"
## Summary

- preserve explicit tool failure metadata across OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic, and Responses inputs
- separate observed attempts from successful planner steps, recover one rejected write with read + bounded retry, preserve the primary write when an auxiliary plan write fails, and stop honestly on repeated or verification failures
- require successful verification output to match the requested bytes before reporting completion
- seed mismatch/unverified responses in all five supported languages and refresh generated closure/self-AST evidence

Closes #905.

## Reproduction and root cause

The reported prompt, `Create a file hello.txt containing exactly: Hello World`, reproduced three defects: the shorter equal-start marker left `exactly:` in the payload; provider error metadata was discarded while every tool result counted as completed; and the general planner emitted success based on call counts without checking result status or output.

The pre-fix focused suite retained in `docs/case-studies/issue-905/test-evidence/regression-red.log` fails 6 of 7 assertions. Both original Agent CLI provider traces and authenticated issue/PR snapshots are retained in the case study. This is a protocol/state bug, so no visual screenshot applies.

## Relationship to `main`

While this branch was open, `main` landed its own fixes for the three defects named in the original issue text: `strip_clause_lead` in `src/agentic_coding/general_planner.rs` (control-phrase parsing) and `ShellEnvelope`/`failed_verification` in `src/agentic_coding/tool_result.rs` (exit-code-aware failure reporting). Those implementations are kept as-is by this merge; the branch's earlier, narrower versions were dropped.

What remains unique to this pull request is the requirement added by the qwen trace in the issue comment — `is_error: true` tool results were being discarded entirely, so the plan advanced over a step that never ran:

- `ChatMessage::is_error` on the shared protocol type, populated from Anthropic `tool_result.is_error`, Responses `is_error`/`isError`/failed `status`, and the OpenAI-compatible `is_error`/`isError` fields
- `Progress` recording observed attempts separately from satisfied steps, so a rejected call cannot count as progress
- the two-turn `read_file` → `write_file` recovery the issue comment asked for, bounded per target path
- content-match verification: literal completion compares transport-normalized command output against the request-derived payload
- `general_execution.rs`, the plan's execution state machine, split out of the planner

## Verification

Latest run, on `06d5a9e2`, which merges `origin/main` (`52ef42e7`, v0.333.0) into the branch:

- `cargo test --test unit` — 2574 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored
- `cargo test --test integration -- --test-threads=1` — 306 passed, 0 failed, 1 ignored (serialized to respect the local runner's socket limit)
- `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`
- `scripts/audit-total-closure.py` — `UNRESOLVED distinct: 0  occurrences: 0`
- `check-file-size`, `check-associative-terminology`, `check-hardcoded-language`, `check-disk-usage-policy`, `check-worker-line-budget`, `check-changelog-fragment` — all passed
- CI on the merged head `06d5a9e2`: CI/CD Pipeline, Coverage, Write-Effect Ladder, Task Ladder, Agentic CLI Matrix, Agent CLI E2E, External Benchmarks, Desktop Release — all eight green

Generated artifacts (self-AST census, planner fixtures, total-closure shards) were regenerated from the merged sources, so they remain pure functions of `src/**` and `data/seed/**`.

Regression coverage includes the exact prompt, 20 parsing variants, Qwen-style explicit errors, Codex-style nonzero exits, bounded write recovery, wrong/matching verification evidence, requested failure vocabulary, and Anthropic/Responses parity. Acceptance traceability is in `docs/case-studies/issue-905/requirements.md`.

## Merge with `main` (v0.333.0)

`main` advanced repeatedly while this branch was open, and each time the pull request went conflicted the conflicts were confined to *generated* artifacts — `data/meta/self-ast/index.lino` and the `data/seed/closure-generated-*.lino` shards. None was ever resolved by hand: `main`'s side was taken and then both artifact families were regenerated from the merged sources (`cargo run --example regenerate_self_ast_census`, `python3 scripts/close-total.py`), so the committed bytes remain exactly what the generators produce. No source file has conflicted at any point.

The final merge (`06d5a9e2`, bringing in `52ef42e7` / v0.333.0) had a single conflict, in `data/meta/self-ast/index.lino`, resolved the same way. The branch is now `MERGEABLE` against `main`.

## The completion claim is now seeded, not English in Rust

The sentence this issue quotes as the false report — `Completed the general change request for … and verified it with …` — was the last outcome of the general-change state machine still typed into `src/` as an English literal, while every failure path (`general_plan_unverified`, `general_plan_verification_mismatch`, `general_plan_repository_planned`) already resolved through the seed. That contradicted the repository's meanings ↔ naturalization rule: a Russian or Chinese request that *succeeded* was answered in English, while the same request that *failed* was answered in its own language.

`general_plan_completed` is now a seeded intent in all five supported languages, rendered through `localized_response`. The English text is byte-identical, so every existing assertion is unchanged; `scripts/hardcoded-language-allowlist.txt` loses exactly one entry, and the five-language response test now covers the completion claim alongside the two failure intents.

## Same-task self-application

Formal AI served the `formal-ai` model to the external Agent CLI in session `ses_034e9dafeffe7nxeTkFhmHLmZN`, which authored and read back the canonical tool-result evidence invariant. That is 1 of 5 reviewed smallest leaves (20%). Session `ses_03474fd81ffeMofsJrUWdYDEEd` refreshed the planner-derived self-healing fixture through the same real client/server path. Replay harnesses and byte-identical artifacts are retained under `experiments/issue_905_*` and `docs/case-studies/issue-905/`.

An earlier whole-issue attempt selected an unsupported hosted fallback and received HTTP 401 before Formal AI executed. It is retained as failed infrastructure evidence and is not counted as authorship; echoed authorization headers are redacted.


## Write-effect ladder regression (fixed)

The issue #916 write-effect ladder, which `main` added while this branch was open, failed rung R916-01 on the first push of the merged branch: `dishonest report=['reported failure although every declared effect landed']`, baseline 9/9 → 8/9.

Root cause: R916-01 replays the exact issue #905 transport fault (`write_stdin failed: Unknown process id 0`, bytes never land). This branch's recovery path exhausted its bounded per-path retries and then reported the transport message directly, without ever running the check the plan itself had named — so the run ended in a failure report that no observed exit code backed.

Fix: once write recovery is exhausted, run the named verification once; its exit status is what the report carries. Locally the ladder is back to 9/9 (`baseline 9/9 -> now 9/9`), and `an_unrecoverable_write_still_asks_the_workspace_before_reporting` pins the behavior in the unit suite.

Summary\n\nIn a single 13-line turn, Formal AI (a) fails both of its `Write` steps with\n`write_stdin failed: Unknown process id 0`, (b) runs its verification command, (c) **sees it exit 1\nwith `No such file or directory`**, and (d) reports:\n\n> Completed the general change request for hello.txt and **verified it** with `cat hello.txt`.\n\nthen ends the turn with `turn.completed`. No file was created. This is a false claim of\nverification against directly observed contrary evidence, and it is the opposite of the guarantee\n#843 established.\n\n## Reproduction\n\nNo wrapper is involved — this is `codex` pointed straight at `formal-ai serve --agent-mode`:\n\n```bash\nformal-ai serve --agent-mode --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8123 &\ncd \"$(mktemp -d)\"\nprintf 'Create a file hello.txt containing exactly: Hello World' | \\\n  env CODEX_HOME=… FORMAL_AI_API_KEY=[REDACTED] codex exec --model formal-ai --json --skip-git-repo-check\n```\n\nFull log:\n[`direct-codex3.log`](https://github.com/link-assistant/hive-mind/blob/main/docs/case-studies/issue-2130/data/runs/direct-codex3.log)\n(13 lines; quoted in full below where relevant). Environment: formal-ai 0.317.0, codex-cli 0.146.0.\n\n```text\n{\"item\":{\"type\":\"agent_message\",\"text\":\"Let me update .formal-ai/general-change-plan.lino for you.\"}}\nERROR codex_core::tools::router: error=write_stdin failed: Unknown process id 0\n{\"item\":{\"type\":\"agent_message\",\"text\":\"Let me update hello.txt for you.\"}}\nERROR codex_core::tools::router: error=write_stdin failed: Unknown process id 0\n{\"item\":{\"type\":\"command_execution\",\"command\":\"/bin/bash -lc 'cat hello.txt'\",\n          \"aggregated_output\":\"cat: hello.txt: No such file or directory\\n\",\n          \"exit_code\":1,\"status\":\"failed\"}}\n{\"item\":{\"type\":\"agent_message\",\"text\":\"Completed the general change request for hello.txt and verified it with `cat hello.txt`. …\"}}\n{\"type\":\"turn.completed\", …}\n```\n\n## Three distinct defects\n\n### 1. `write_stdin failed: Unknown process id 0` — the Write capability never executes\n\nBoth `Write` steps fail with process id `0`, which is not a real pid. Whatever dispatches `Write`\nis being handed an uninitialised process handle. Because the failures are logged to stderr rather\nthan surfaced as step failures, the plan proceeds as if both writes succeeded.\n\n### 2. The step outcome is not consulted before claiming success\n\n`item_3` is `\"status\":\"failed\"` with `\"exit_code\":1` and output `No such file or directory`. The very\nnext message claims completion *and* verification. The exit code is present in the same structured\nrecord that the summary is generated from, so this is a matter of reading it, not of inferring it.\n\nThis is the most serious of the three: a system whose stated principle is \"I do not return a program\nI cannot derive\" should equally refuse to report an unobserved success. #843 fixed the phrasing of\nhonest failures; this path bypasses it by not recognising that a failure occurred.\n\n### 3. Verification evidence is extracted by naive string slicing\n\n```text\nstep 3\n  capability \"Run\"\n  action \"run the request-derived verification command\"\n  expected_evidence \"exactly: Hello World\"\n  command \"cat hello.txt\"\n```\n\nThe request was *\"…containing exactly: Hello World\"*. The expected evidence became the literal\nstring `exactly: Hello World` — the word \"exactly\" is an adverb modifying the requirement, not part\nof the content. Had the write succeeded, verification would have compared against the wrong string\nand failed. The same class of mistake shows up in language routing (filed separately): *\"…whose\nentire content is **the** single line\"* is parsed as language `\"the\"`.\n\n## Suggested fix\n\n1. **Fail the step when the capability fails.** `write_stdin failed` must mark the step failed and\n   stop the plan, not log to stderr and continue. Separately, find why the process id is `0` — the\n   Write path appears to run before the process it writes to is created.\n2. **Gate the success message on observed evidence.** Before emitting \"Completed … and verified it\n   with `X`\", require that `X` ran and exited 0, and that its output matched `expected_evidence`. If\n   either is absent, the honest terminal state is \"attempted, not verified\" — which #843's\n   vocabulary already supports.\n3. **Stop deriving `expected_evidence` by slicing after a keyword.** Extract the content as the span\n   the request delimits (quoted text, a code block, or the remainder after a colon *with* leading\n   adverbs stripped), and when the span is ambiguous, ask rather than guess. A regression corpus of\n   ~20 phrasings of \"create a file containing X\" would pin this down cheaply.\n\n## Related\n\n- Downstream: link-assistant/hive-mind#2130\n- Honesty guarantee this path bypasses: #843\n- Agent-mode no-op with self-referential verification: #904\n- Wrapper argv/provider defects from the same investigation: #902, #903\n- Full analysis: [case study §6.4](https://github.com/link-assistant/hive-mind/blob/main/docs/case-studies/issue-2130/README.md)\n","closed_by":{"login":"konard","id":1431904,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjE0MzE5MDQ=","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/1431904?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard","html_url":"https://github.com/konard","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"reactions":{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/link-assistant/formal-ai/issues/905/reactions","total_count":0,"+1":0,"-1":0,"laugh":0,"hooray":0,"confused":0,"heart":0,"rocket":0,"eyes":0},"timeline_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/link-assistant/formal-ai/issues/905/timeline","performed_via_github_app":null,"state_reason":"completed","pinned_comment":null}
